# COMSOL-Grade FEM Frequency-Domain Solver Implementation Masterplan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use `superpowers:subagent-driven-development` or `superpowers:executing-plans` when implementing tasks from this plan. Keep this file updated after each accepted implementation step.

**Goal:** Build a COMSOL-class FEM frequency-domain product in Fullmag: driven harmonic response, modal/eigenfrequency analysis, FMR spectra, dispersion, periodic/Floquet workflows, complex field visualization, and provenance-preserving CPU/GPU runtime gates.

**Architecture:** Frequency-domain is an analysis family, not one solver. Fullmag keeps `FrequencyResponse` as the forced harmonic solver and `Eigenmodes` as the modal/eigenfrequency solver; both share the linearized LLG tangent operator, equilibrium provenance, boundary-condition contracts, artifact family, and Control Room resource model.

**Tech Stack:** Python DSL and canonical script export, `ProblemIR`, FEM planner, Rust runner orchestration, native FEM under `backends/fem` with MFEM/hypre/libCEED/CUDA and PETSc/SLEPc-class modal targets, v2 resource-first API, Control Room Explorer/Inspector/Analysis/Viewport modules, JSON control-plane artifacts, Zarr heavy-data payloads, and container-backed `just` verification recipes.

---

## 0. Status Of This Document

- Created: 2026-06-30.
- Scope: living masterplan and progress ledger for the FEM frequency-domain solver program.
- Location: `docs/plans/active/frequency-domain-fem-masterplan-2026-06-11/11-comsol-grade-frequency-domain-masterplan-2026-06-30.md`.
- Update rule: every implementation step that changes capability, backend behavior, artifacts, frontend behavior, verification gates, or user-facing status must update this file in the same change.

Progress percentages below are engineering readiness estimates for the named milestone acceptance gates. They are not marketing claims. A milestone is not production-complete until its exit gates pass through the managed/container-backed `just` recipes where native FEM/MFEM/CUDA/hypre/libCEED runtime behavior is involved.

### 1.5 COMSOL reference

- `docs/comsol/Manual_for_Micromagnetics_Module.pdf`

This PDF is a parity reference for user-facing workflow expectations, not a semantic source that overrides Fullmag physics notes.

Relevant COMSOL-facing requirements extracted for this plan:

- Time-domain micromagnetics and frequency-domain micromagnetics are separate study surfaces.
- Frequency-domain micromagnetics is linearized LLG around equilibrium `m0`.
- Dynamic magnetization `delta_m` is a complex phasor.
- The harmonic factor is supplied by the solver; users specify the dynamic drive amplitude/phase, not a hand-written sinusoid.
- Equilibrium `m0` must be stable and may come from a prior time-domain or relaxation solve.
- `Eigenfrequency` and `Frequency Domain` are separate studies: eigenfrequency returns natural modes/eigenvectors; frequency domain returns forced response at requested drive frequencies.
- Periodic and Floquet periodic boundary conditions are first-class frequency-domain features.
- Floquet uses phase `exp(-i k dot (r_dst - r_src))`, matching Fullmag's `exp_minus_i_k_dot_delta_r` convention.
- COMSOL-style nonzero-k dispersion maps to `Eigenmodes`: solve an eigenfrequency problem on the minimal Floquet cell for each swept `k_F`, then assemble branches over the k path. It is not a time-domain pulse/FFT workflow and not a `FrequencyResponse` driven-response smoke.

## 2. Non-Negotiable Product Split

Fullmag uses one analysis family and two solver products:

| Name | Canonical study | Mathematical form | Primary output | Status owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Frequency-domain family | umbrella only | shared linearized tangent LLG contract | manifest, resources, workflow routing | this masterplan and artifact specs |
| Driven response | `FrequencyResponse` | `(i omega B - A) q = b` | response sweep, susceptibility, absorbed power, complex response fields | driven-response milestones |
| Modal/eigenfrequency | `Eigenmodes` | `A q = lambda B q` or equivalent gyrotropic pencil | eigenfrequencies, modes, dispersion, branch tracking | modal milestones |

Rules:

- Do not call `Eigenmodes` the driven frequency-domain solver.
- Do not call response-sweep peaks eigenmodes. They are mode candidates or peak-derived response modes.
- Do not promote modal support based on driven-response artifacts.
- Do not promote driven response based on eigen artifacts.
- Do not present dense/reference or development smoke paths as COMSOL-class production execution.
- Do not promote any new frequency-domain lane until the audit P0 mathematical contract is closed: one `exp(i omega t)` convention, correct gyrotropic/eigenvalue mapping, dynamic demag Poisson sign, `Ms`-correct susceptibility and absorbed-power units, and tangent-frame transport for PBC/Floquet.
- Do not classify magnetization-only periodic projection as magnetostatic PBC. If `m` is periodic but `phi`/`H_demag` behave like a finite isolated airbox, the result is false or incomplete PBC and cannot promote `periodic_airbox_k0` demag.

## 3. Current Implementation Inventory

### 3.1 Python DSL and canonical script export

Progress: 85%.

Implemented:

- `packages/fullmag-py/src/fullmag/model/study.py` defines `Eigenmodes` and `FrequencyResponse`.
- `packages/fullmag-py/src/fullmag/model/eigen.py` owns `KPoint`, `KPath`, `ModeTracking`, and k-sampling serialization helpers.
- `packages/fullmag-py/src/fullmag/world.py` exposes flat and staged helpers for `eigenmodes` and `frequency_response`.
- `packages/fullmag-py/src/fullmag/runtime/script_builder.py` exports and reloads eigen and frequency-response stages, including spin-wave boundary configuration.
- Builder draft export, scene-document overrides, and canonical script rewrite now preserve production CPU modal dispersion authoring for `target="frequency_window"`, `frequency_min`/`frequency_max`, `operator="full_2x2"`, no-demag Floquet `KPath`, and `fm.FloquetBC(["x_faces"])` from `examples/fem_eigenmodes_dispersion_window_k_path.py`; text-valued builder overrides for the edited frequency window are applied back into the canonical script.
- Builder text round-trip now preserves closed reciprocal-space `KPath` loops with `closed=true`, so UI/script export edits cannot silently turn a closed Brillouin-zone loop into an open `len(points)-1` path.

Remaining:

- Authoring/UI fields must stay aligned with canonical Python names. Compatibility aliases such as `near_frequency` must remain boundary translations, not backend semantics.
- Public examples must avoid implying unsupported dynamic demag or general Floquet production.
- More end-to-end round-trip tests are needed for the remaining frequency-window eigenfrequency and response-map authoring variants.

### 3.2 ProblemIR and planner

Progress: 74%.

Implemented:

- `crates/fullmag-ir/src/study.rs` and companion contract files represent `Eigenmodes` and `FrequencyResponse`.
- `crates/fullmag-plan/src/fem.rs` plans FEM eigenmodes and current FEM frequency-response slices.
- Planner rejects unsupported FDM response, dynamic demag-k, unsupported Floquet/demag combinations, missing periodic pair metadata, and unsupported GPU slices.
- Capability matrix now distinguishes partial driven-response slices from semantic-only modal interior-window status.

Remaining:

- Frequency-window eigenfrequency authoring is still semantic/public-contract level; production scalable eigensolver completion is pending.
- Capability payloads need to remain synchronized with the newest accepted CPU/GPU Floquet slices.
- Planner messages and UI capability reasons need explicit mapping for every accepted/rejected slice.
- Audit P0 must be reflected in capability reasons: unsupported modal/response lanes should cite missing mathematical contracts where the gyrotropic pencil, dynamic demag derivative, susceptibility/absorbed-power units, or tangent-frame transport are not yet implemented.

### 3.3 Native FEM backend, CPU driven response

Progress: 72%.

Implemented:

- `backends/fem/include/frequency_domain/*` defines frequency-domain contracts, tangent frame, operator terms, excitation, driven-response request, modal request/result, solver progress, and C ABI-facing structures.
- `backends/fem/src/frequency_domain/*` implements shared contract glue, tangent/equilibrium utilities, driven-response solve orchestration, progress JSON, and structured unavailable/error result handling.
- `backends/fem/cpu/frequency_domain/*` contains dense validation response, MFEM operator context, exchange, Zeeman, DMI, tangent-space machinery, production CPU driven response, modal operator payloads, window partitioning, contour interval solver support, mode filtering, and mode deduplication utilities.
- `crates/fullmag-runner/src/frequency_response.rs` builds native payloads, preserves requested/resolved execution, writes response artifacts, and prevents unsupported forced GPU paths from silently falling back.
- CPU `gamma/free` and `k=0 static-periodic` magnetic response slices are partial production executable.
- CPU periodic-airbox dynamic-demag work has a qualified matrix-free driven-response diagnostic slice with solved single-point artifacts for the current 2.75 GHz smoke gate.
- The periodic-antidot relax-then-frequency-driven managed headless target now writes to a stable report artifact root, declares explicit `ProblemIR.pbc` intent through `study.pbc(x=True, y=True, demag="periodic_airbox_k0")`, and the native CPU GMRES response fallback no longer uses the old debug `256/32` or too-tight `4096/4096` iteration/restart defaults.

Remaining:

- Periodic-airbox `include_demag=true` driven response has a solved single-point CPU diagnostic gate, but is not solved to production acceptance yet.
- The current periodic-airbox path must be described as a qualified driven-response diagnostic slice until stable M5-accepted relaxation, refined spectrum, and supercell acceptance all pass.
- Nonzero-k dynamic demag/Floquet coupled `delta_m/delta_phi` is not implemented.
- CPU dynamic demag must gain a stronger preconditioner/Schur strategy instead of relying on arbitrary iteration-limit increases.
- Stable antidot frequency-driven artifacts are now available for inspection, but the full seven-point example is still a long CPU solve and is not itself a fast acceptance gate.

### 3.4 Native FEM backend, GPU driven response

Progress: 72%.

Implemented:

- `backends/fem/gpu/cuda/frequency_domain/driven_response_gpu.cu` implements a CUDA tangent operator for exchange, local precession/mass, Zeeman, uniaxial anisotropy, and damping-style local terms.
- The runner exposes strict GPU request paths without silent CPU/dense validation fallback.
- GPU `gamma/free` no-demag driven response is partial production executable.
- GPU `k=0 static-periodic` no-demag response is partial production executable when periodic pair metadata is complete.
- The periodic-antidot relax-then-frequency-driven managed target now runs GPU relaxation followed by GPU `k=0 static-periodic` no-demag response on the compacted magnetic-node slice of a shared-domain mesh, with artifact validation requiring `production_gpu` and `static_periodic_projection`.
- The managed CPU/GPU static-periodic no-demag parity gate passes for the small magnetic-body smoke fixture using the artifact validator's `--compare-reference` check.
- The managed CPU/GPU shared-domain static-periodic no-demag parity gate passes for the compacted magnetic-node slice of a shared-domain airbox smoke fixture using the artifact validator's `--compare-reference` check.
- A narrow nonzero-k Floquet no-demag development smoke exists for explicit GPU, magnetic-body, no-demag/no-DMI requests with complete pair metadata and Bloch-phased tangent drive.
- `just verify-fem-frequency-domain-gpu-floquet-runtime` and related recipes exist as managed runtime gates for current development slices.

Remaining:

- GPU dynamic demag is absent.
- GPU periodic-airbox dynamic demag must explicitly report unavailable and must not silently run CPU.
- GPU DMI in frequency response remains gated for current promoted slices.
- Nonzero-k Floquet GPU path is phase-projected no-demag smoke, not full Bloch-reduced production response.
- Broader production labels still require target-antidot parity and term coverage beyond the current small magnetic-body and small shared-domain compacted-slice smokes.

### 3.5 Native FEM backend, modal/eigenfrequency

Progress: 42%.

Implemented:

- `backends/fem/include/frequency_domain/modal_eigen_request.hpp` and `modal_eigen_result.hpp` define modal request/result contracts.
- `backends/fem/cpu/frequency_domain/slepc_modal_eigen.*` and tests expose SLEPc/PETSc dependency diagnostics and tiny shift-invert validation behavior when dependencies are available.
- `backends/fem/cpu/frequency_domain/production_cpu_modal_eigen.cpp`, contour interval, spectral transform, window partition, and mode filtering files exist as the current native modal spine.
- `crates/fullmag-runner/src/eigen/*` writes v2 eigen spectrum, branch, dispersion, mode metadata, and Zarr-backed mode payload artifacts.
- `just verify-fem-frequency-domain-eigen-runtime` verifies the current eigen artifact contract for `examples/fem_eigenmodes.py` and requires production shift-invert provenance for the free-boundary `examples/fem_eigenmodes_frequency_window.py` window artifacts.
- The modal dispersion contract is `Eigenmodes` plus `KSamplingIR::Single` or `KSamplingIR::Path`, `spin_wave_bc.kind="floquet"`, branch-tracked `eigen/dispersion.csv`, and mode payloads keyed by `k_vector`/`path_s_rad_per_m`.
- The multi-k runner now treats `dispersion_curve` / Python `save("dispersion")`
  as the public dispersion artifact request: k-path bookkeeping can still
  write spectrum, branch, diagnostics, and manifest metadata, but
  `eigen/dispersion.csv`, legacy branch-table CSV, and dispersion path metadata
  are published only for requested dispersion outputs. A dispersion-only k-path
  request publishes the default branch/mode range `0..count-1`, so the curve is
  usable even without a separate `save("mode")`.
- The multi-k CPU adapter now routes each k-path sample through
  `execute_cpu_fem_eigen`, so eligible gamma-equivalent samples can reach the
  production SLEPc selected-spectrum / shift-invert entrypoint instead of being
  forced through the baseline reference solve. Nonzero-k/Floquet production
  modal samples remain gated until the production Bloch/Floquet operator exists.
- `examples/fem_eigenmodes_production_gamma_k_path.py` plus
  `just verify-fem-frequency-domain-eigen-production-gamma-k-path-runtime`
  prove the gamma-only multi-k bridge through the managed production CPU modal
  adapter. Fresh artifacts report `solver_model="slepc_multi_shift_invert_production_cpu_dense"`,
  `execution_lane="production_cpu"`, `spectral_transform="shift_invert"`,
  `sample_count=3`, `capabilities.production_native_solver_available=true`,
  `capabilities.validation_artifact=false`, and remapped `sample_0002` mode-field
  payloads. This is a production-adapter/provenance gate only; it is not
  nonzero-k Floquet dispersion.
- The CPU/reference `Full2x2` Floquet exchange operator now scales FEM stiffness by `2A/(mu0 Ms)` in scalar/full2x2 and real/complex modal assembly paths, and a focused regression checks the exchange-only analytic dispersion scale for `k = 0, 1e7, 2e7 rad/m`.
- Production-facing dispersion validation must also include physically typical
  narrow DE/BV sweeps rather than only broad k-direction scans or high-k stress
  checks: Damon-Eshbach with in-plane `k` perpendicular to equilibrium
  magnetization, backward-volume with in-plane `k` parallel to equilibrium
  magnetization, `|k| <= 2e6..3e6 rad/m` (`2..3 1/um`), and a low-GHz modal
  window such as `0..5 GHz`, compared with the applicable analytic dispersion
  for the documented material, film thickness, bias field, demag model, and
  boundary assumptions.
- The default scientific acceptance test for dispersion is therefore not an
  all-direction k-space map. It is a compact DE/BV matrix:

  | Fixture | Equilibrium magnetization | Swept wave vector | Default range | Frequency window | Required comparison |
  |---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
  | Damon-Eshbach | in-plane `m0` | in-plane `k perpendicular m0` | `0..2e6` to `3e6 rad/m` (`0..2` to `3 1/um`) | `0..5 GHz` unless the material/bias narrows it | analytic thin-film DE dispersion for the declared film/material/bias/demag assumptions |
  | Backward-volume | in-plane `m0` | in-plane `k parallel m0` | `0..2e6` to `3e6 rad/m` (`0..2` to `3 1/um`) | `0..5 GHz` unless the material/bias narrows it | analytic thin-film BV dispersion for the declared film/material/bias/demag assumptions |

  Broader direction scans, high-k exchange-only checks, and Brillouin-zone
  stress paths remain useful diagnostics, but they cannot replace this narrow
  DE/BV analytic acceptance gate. Runtime artifacts must preserve the geometry,
  k-range, frequency window, analytic model, branch IDs, and relative-error
  tolerance so the validator and Control Room can show the numerical curve
  against the analytic reference rather than only plotting solver points.
- The default regression policy follows the same experimental workflow:
  parameterize DE and BV separately, keep the sampled wave-vector range near
  `0..2e6` to `3e6 rad/m`, keep the requested modal/frequency window within the
  low-GHz band such as `0..5 GHz`, and compare every accepted scenario point to
  the declared analytic model. Tests over many k directions or larger
  Brillouin-zone paths are stress/exploration coverage, not the normal DE/BV
  scientific acceptance gate.
- The low-k DE/BV artifact contract now carries that comparison directly in
  `eigen/dispersion.csv`: `analytic_frequency_hz`, `relative_error`, and
  `validation_geometry` are required for validated DE/BV scenario samples, and
  Control Room's shared dispersion chart model preserves them as an analytic
  overlay/error channel instead of forcing users to compare curves outside the
  artifact pipeline.
- The shared eigen artifact plotter now consumes the same DE/BV analytic
  columns: `examples/dyspersje.png` can be regenerated from the managed
  low-k DE/BV bundle with the numerical branch and analytic reference overlay
  on the same k-path axis plus the maximum published relative error in the
  footer.
- `examples/fem_eigenmodes_dispersion_k_path.py` now demonstrates a lowest-branch no-demag reference k-path with a magnetic cell touching the periodic `x` faces, so the managed dispersion CSV produces a non-flat reference curve instead of an isolated-body/airbox-only phase artifact. The reference and production-window managed examples now use a low-k reciprocal `G-X-G--X` range with `|k| = 2e6 rad/m`, matching the normal plotting range better than the earlier high-k stress path while still remaining no-demag exchange-only evidence.
- `scripts/verify_fem_frequency_domain_eigen_artifacts.py --require-reference-full-2x2-floquet` now rejects gamma-only and flat reference artifacts by requiring at least three samples, one nonzero `k`, strictly increasing `path_s`, labelled endpoints, and a non-flat branch-frequency span.
- The managed no-demag exchange+Zeeman k-path gate now also checks the branch frequency scale against `f(k)=gamma0*(|H0| + 2A|k|^2/(mu0 Ms))/(2pi)` from runtime `metadata.json`, so order-of-magnitude exchange-unit regressions cannot pass by merely being non-flat.
- `just verify-fem-frequency-domain-eigen-dispersion-runtime` and the production-window `just verify-fem-frequency-domain-eigen-dispersion-window-runtime` now regenerate `examples/dyspersje.png` from validated managed `eigen/dispersion.csv` bundles through the shared eigen artifact plotter, and the plotter validates that the PNG is readable, sufficiently sized, finite, visible, and nonblank. The example image is therefore reproducible from the same runtime artifacts that pass the reference and production CPU modal k-path gates.
- Native modal diagnostics now preserve an explicit modal `k_vector_rad_m` and `k_vector_len` through the shared modal diagnostics wrapper when the Rust runner forwards a gamma-point `KSamplingIR::Single([0,0,0])` through the production CPU modal ABI, including sparse validation-error diagnostics.
- The runner-side production modal dense gyrotropic generalized pencil now uses
  the `exp(i omega t)` contract explicitly: physical `G` is distinguished from
  the solver right-hand-side pencil `B=-G`, diagnostics report
  `gyrotropic_form="pencil_B=-G=[[0,M],[-M,0]]"`, and a one-node macrospin
  sign guard prevents regressing to the ambiguous `K phi = lambda G phi` form.
- The runner-side native modal handoff now also validates the accepted
  positive-frequency branch for `lambda=i omega`: `omega_rad_s` must equal
  `Im(lambda)`, `frequency_hz` must equal `Im(lambda)/(2*pi)`, and a one-node
  macrospin residual guard checks the same `K phi = lambda B phi`, `B=-G`
  pencil rather than only checking the matrix sign.
- The modal artifact validator now enforces the same
  `lambda_eq_i_omega` per-mode contract on `eigen/spectrum.v2.json`,
  per-mode metadata, and `eigen/metadata/eigen_summary.json`, so artifact
  bundles cannot pass if the accepted branch, `omega_rad_s`, and
  `frequency_hz` drift apart.
- Native production CPU modal diagnostics now reject nonzero-k Floquet selected-spectrum requests with the concrete missing contract: `required_operator_contract="bloch_floquet_tangent_operator_with_periodic_pairs"` and `required_operator_payload_kind="bloch_floquet_tangent_operator"` unless the native request supplies a dense modal payload labelled `payload_kind="bloch_floquet_tangent_operator"` plus Floquet periodic pairs. The native modal C ABI also carries the Floquet k-vector, phase convention, and Floquet periodic-pair tail; direct native requests with supplied pairs report `modal_periodic_pair_contract_available=true` and `floquet_periodic_pair_count`. The Rust native modal wrapper and FEM eigen runner can now build that pair payload from selected `mesh.periodic_node_pairs` plus boundary-pair translations for Floquet requests, with `phase_rad=-k dot translation`, and the managed windowed k-path target now exercises that production CPU payload path for the no-demag `Full2x2` slice.
- Reference/MVP nonzero-k Floquet windowed k-path artifacts now carry the same missing-operator contract in runner solver diagnostics, and the eigen artifact validator rejects reference windowed Floquet bundles that publish the production CPU rejection reason without `required_operator_contract="bloch_floquet_tangent_operator_with_periodic_pairs"` and `modal_periodic_pair_contract_available=false`.
- `/v2/sessions/current/analysis/frequency-domain/manifest.v1` now exposes modal
  dispersion lanes separately as `capabilities.dispersion.reference_cpu`,
  `capabilities.dispersion.production_cpu`,
  `capabilities.dispersion.production_cpu_gamma_k_path`, and
  `capabilities.dispersion.production_gpu`, so Control Room can show the
  current CPU reference path and gamma-equivalent production CPU provenance
  bridge without implying nonzero-k production CPU/GPU support.

Remaining:

- The production gyrotropic pencil sign, accepted positive-frequency branch,
  local macrospin residual, and `Im(lambda)` frequency mapping are now guarded
  at the runner-side generalized-pencil boundary and in modal artifact
  validation, but full selected-spectrum promotion still needs end-to-end
  native/SLEPc residual, damping, linewidth, normalization, and scalability
  coverage.
- COMSOL-class large-object eigenfrequency still requires sparse/matrix-free selected-spectrum scaling beyond the current managed free-boundary dense-adapter gate, plus production residuals, orthogonality, mass normalization, spectral-transform provenance, and live progress.
- Modal dynamic demag and Floquet/PBC eigensolve are not production-complete.
- Modal Bloch/Floquet k-path dispersion remains a separate capability from the GPU driven-response Floquet smoke. Current evidence is a reference/small modal artifact path, a lowest-branch managed k-path example, and a narrow analytic exchange regression; it is not production selected-spectrum CPU or GPU dispersion.
- Native modal GPU dispersion is unavailable until a real modal GPU eigensolver and matching Floquet operator exist. Forced GPU modal-dispersion requests must fail explicitly rather than reuse CPU or response-smoke paths.
- Dense/reference modal paths must remain validation/reference paths until scalable native modal gates pass.
- UI must keep modal/eigenfrequency labels separate from driven response.

- Both modal artifact writer paths now satisfy the hardened contract: the
  generic `eigen/artifacts.rs` path and the current `fem_eigen.rs` runtime path
  publish seed-only tracking provenance for single-sample eigen bundles,
  mode-field handoff columns in `eigen/dispersion.csv`, and tracking diagnostics
  in the frequency-domain manifest.
- `just verify-fem-frequency-domain-eigen-runtime` now passes after rebuilding
  the managed FEM runtime and validating both `examples/fem_eigenmodes.py` and
  `examples/fem_eigenmodes_frequency_window.py` against
  `scripts/verify_fem_frequency_domain_eigen_artifacts.py`.
- `scripts/verify_fem_frequency_domain_supercell_artifacts.py` compares unit-cell periodic-airbox response artifacts with Gamma-like supercell artifacts.
- `scripts/derive_fem_frequency_response_modes.py` derives a response-peak mode artifact from a completed response sweep.
- `scripts/fem_frequency_response_refinement_env.py` computes refined sweep frequency env values from prior artifacts.
- Static PBC-demag runtime artifacts now distinguish the physical magnetostatic boundary model from the underlying airbox realization: accepted PBC runs must publish `demag_runtime.magnetostatic_boundary_model = "periodic_airbox_k0"`, `poisson_operator = "pbc_reduced_poisson"`, and `periodic_reduction.method = "P^T A P"` with positive periodic node/boundary-pair counts, per-pair-id counts matching `metadata.mesh`, and Robin exclusion of periodic boundary markers.
- Driven-response artifact validation now rejects drift in both allowed
  observable provenance modes. The legacy no-`Ms` path remains explicitly
  labelled as a `delta_m_over_h_drive` proxy in `m/A`, while the native path
  with a valid `Ms` contract emits `delta_M_over_h_drive` dimensionless SI
  susceptibility and `W/m^3` absorbed-power density with `mu0 * Ms` provenance.

Remaining:

- Periodic-airbox solved bundle must pass the same observability/provenance requirements as bounded solve-error bundles.
- Artifact validators must be kept in lockstep with any schema evolution; `model="airbox"` plus `boundary_variant="robin"` is not sufficient evidence of magnetostatic PBC unless the periodic-reduction provenance is present.
- Zarr remains the production heavy-data direction; raw bin payloads are compatibility only.
- Response and modal artifacts must include the audit-required units/provenance fields for `delta_m` vs `delta_M`, susceptibility, absorbed power, phasor convention, matrix form, and response-derived peak modes.

### 3.7 API and Control Room frontend

Progress: 85%.

Implemented:

- `crates/fullmag-api/src/router_v2/handlers/analysis/eigen.rs`, `response.rs`, and `frequency_domain.rs` expose v2 analysis routes.
- `apps/control-room/src/kernel/api/apiPaths.ts` and `ControlRoomApi.ts` contain typed route constants and facade methods for eigen and frequency-domain response resources.
- `apps/control-room/src/kernel/resources/studyRuntimeResources.ts` exposes resource hooks for manifest, eigen spectrum/branches/diagnostics/dispersion/mode/field metadata, response sweep/progress/cancel/diagnostics/frequency point/field metadata.
- `apps/control-room/src/modules/explorer/builders/frequencyDomainExplorerNodes.ts` builds a COMSOL-like frequency-domain result tree: calculation modes, FMR, dispersion, eigen, response, comparison, exports, periodic pairs, response fields, and response-map only when artifacts prove it exists.
- `apps/control-room/src/modules/inspector/panels/FrequencyDomainInspectorPanel.tsx` and `frequency-domain/FrequencyDomainResultInspectors.tsx` provide dedicated panels for calculation modes, FMR, modal spectrum, modes, dispersion, branches, response sweep, response points, diagnostics, exports, and periodic pair resources.
- `apps/control-room/src/modules/analysis-plots/*` and `apps/control-room/src/shared/domain/analysis/frequencyDomainChartModels.ts` build spectrum, dispersion, branch, response, peak, and modal-vs-driven comparison series.
- The shared dispersion chart model preserves canonical `line_width_hz` from
  `eigen/dispersion.csv` as per-point modal linewidth, matching the backend
  damping/linewidth artifact contract instead of dropping it at the frontend
  boundary.
- Dispersion chart point selection promotes rows with `mode_field_id` to
  `results.eigen.mode` selections carrying the mode-field vector resource, so
  Control Room can hand off selected dispersion points to the 3D mode overlay
  instead of stopping at the dispersion CSV.
- `apps/control-room/src/kernel/visualization/AnalysisFieldOverlayController.ts` and `analysisFieldOverlayCommandContributions.ts` support eigen-mode and frequency-response complex field overlays with real, imaginary, absolute, phase, and phase-rotated-real views.
- `apps/control-room/src/modules/footer/FooterTelemetry.tsx` now renders a
  frequency-domain stage progress bar from backend-owned resources instead of
  generic `step/t/dt` telemetry for long frequency-domain solves. Driven
  response stages show current sweep point, GHz range, GMRES iteration, and
  residual from `stage_execution` plus `response/progress`; modal/eigen stages
  show active eigensolver phase, percent, solver/DOF, iteration, and residual
  from `stage_execution`.
- Native driven-response progress now also publishes the current GMRES
  iteration budget and a bounded current-frequency solve fraction, allowing
  terminal and Control Room progress bars to advance during a long single
  frequency solve instead of waiting for the next completed frequency point.
- `crates/fullmag-cli/src/orchestrator.rs` maps native
  `fem_frequency_response_progress` live scalar updates into
  `stage_execution.progress_*`, so Control Room does not have to infer driven
  sweep progress from scalar time-step rows.
- Frequency-domain solver diagnostics now surface the modal
  `production_cpu_rejection_reason` and `production_cpu_rejection_scope` from
  eigen diagnostics artifacts, so a reference nonzero-k Floquet dispersion path
  is not reduced to a generic unsupported/available label in Control Room.
- The Study inspector authoring model now round-trips Python builder
  `eigen_k_path` text for modal dispersion as a local `kPath` draft field,
  serializes it back to `eigen_k_path`, and treats that path as nonzero-k
  modal dispersion when inferring the active calculation mode.
- The Control Room explorer and Study authoring smoke now treat saved
  `eigen_k_path` text as a modal k-path sampling node: the transaction-backed
  Eigenmodes stage editor can add a k-path, save `eigen_k_path`, reveal the
  `k-Path` child node, and inspect the saved value through the browser flow.

Remaining:

- Browser smoke proof is still required before claiming viewport overlay completion for new visual behavior.
- UI authoring must expose every Python-authorable frequency-domain field through transaction-backed Study inspector controls, not result-only previews; the current browser proof covers the edited modal-dispersion `eigen_k_path` slice, but broader frequency-domain authoring coverage remains open.
- Capability labels must distinguish reference, partial production, unsupported, and unavailable backend lanes without hiding no-demag/demag limits.
- Full Control Room gates remain: `pnpm --dir apps/control-room typecheck`, `lint`, `test`, plus browser smoke for viewport overlay changes.

### 3.8 Static PBC equilibrium and demag stabilization

Progress: 76%.

Implemented:

- `docs/physics/0800-fem-static-pbc-demag.md` defines the static/time-domain FEM PBC demag contract for periodic magnetic samples with open airbox direction.
- The active PBC/Bloch plan names the 200 x 200 x 10 nm thin-film antidot unit cell with a centered 50 nm hole as the target magnonic-crystal smoke geometry.
- Periodic magnetic node-pair metadata, frozen magnetic submesh preparation, shared-airbox materialization, and supercell artifact comparison scripts exist.
- Static PBC demag has reference/source-visible evidence for reduced periodic classes, open-axis Robin treatment, periodic-pair diagnostics, and primitive-vs-supercell artifact checks.
- The ordinary managed periodic-antidot CPU and GPU relaxation gates now pass for both `exchange_coupled` and `air_gap` fixtures with same-step `m`, gauge-adjusted `phi`, periodic `H_demag`, balanced normal `B` flux, no artificial side magnetic charge, finite torque/energy telemetry, and CPU/GPU provenance. This closes the previously observed false-PBC `H_demag` seam regression, but it does not close strict M5 production acceptance until z-padding and primitive-vs-supercell reports are supplied for the same accepted workload.
- Strict M5 now has artifact-backed report entry points for z-padding, central-cell extraction, primitive-vs-supercell comparison, preparatory repeated-unit initial-state generation, and a diagnostic managed run that consumes that sampled state through a headless initial-state override with artifact/live metadata provenance. `verify-fem-static-pbc-demag-equilibrium-runtime` rejects missing z-padding plus at least one controlled supercell comparison report before running the ordinary CPU/GPU relaxation gates, the report writer rejects self-comparison or incompatible-workload artifact roots, and the periodic-antidot validator requires report `workload` to match the accepted run and independently rejects over-threshold metrics even when a report declares `status="ok"`. The repeated-state validator path also requires `initial_magnetization_state_override` provenance, a resolvable JSON source state, matching vector counts, and component-wise agreement between the source state and artifact `m_initial.json`. Repeated-state supercell reports now carry `supercell_initial_magnetization_state_override`, and the strict runtime validator can require that report through `FULLMAG_PBC_RELAX_REPEATED_STATE_SUPERCELL_REPORT`. The supercell report now also requires explicit `fem_static_pbc_supercell_central_cell.v1` extraction provenance. The default extractor selects central-cell nodes from `mesh/node_geometry.v1.json`, computes central-cell `E_demag` by element-centroid magnetic tetrahedron integration, and computes central-cell torque from exported `H_eff`, so global supercell averages cannot masquerade as central-cell agreement. The report also gates relaxation-state comparability through `relaxation_state_mean_deviation_relative_error`, so a failing primitive-vs-3x3 run can distinguish operator/PBC mismatch from a different independently relaxed central-cell state. A separate `--state initial` supercell comparison mode and `verify-fem-static-pbc-demag-supercell-initial-state-artifacts` target now compare `m_initial.json` plus the first `H_demag`/`demag_phi` field snapshot for operator-level primitive-vs-supercell checks without incorrectly gating final `E_demag` or torque metadata. The 2026-07-01 managed ordinary primitive-vs-3x3 report is now recorded as diagnostic `status="failed"` evidence (`e_demag_density_relative_error=5.183127e-02`, `h_demag_stats_relative_error=3.049715e-02`, large mesh-count mismatch), and the repeated-state wrapper no longer lets that diagnostic report prevent the controlled repeated-state gate from running. The repeated-unit initial-state producer is strict by default and currently exposes that the independent 3x3 remesh is not node-identical to the primitive mesh, so the new repeated-state runtime target is diagnostic preparation for a controlled/frozen fixture rather than M5 closure. A fresh 2026-07-01 managed supercell relaxation run after the Robin open-axis-radius fix reached step 530 with `max_steps=4000` and continued oscillating near the torque threshold, so full independent 3x3 relaxation remains a long equilibrium proof rather than a practical iteration gate.
- The repeated-state `final_to_initial` comparator now reports constant-field and affine-potential residual diagnostics. On the regenerated failing report, subtracting the best mean `H_demag` delta leaves `p99_relative_error_after_mean_delta=2.164383e-01`, and the best affine `demag_phi` fit leaves `max_abs_delta_after_affine_A=1.069810e-04`; therefore the current M5 blocker is not a removable field offset or scalar-potential gauge trend.

Remaining:

- Keep the explicit managed gate for static PBC equilibrium of the antidot unit cell before any response or eigenfrequency run consumes the equilibrium.
- Promote equilibrium acceptance by physical checks, not by short smoke completion: torque residual, energy monotonicity or stable descent, `|m|=1`, seam continuity, demag energy sign/convergence, and absence of hidden finite-airbox fallback.
- Compare the PBC unit cell against an explicit 2x2 or 3x3 supercell for average magnetization, demag energy density, `H_demag` statistics, seam mismatch, and central-cell torque residual.
- Publish the accepted equilibrium, static demag diagnostics, periodic-pair summary, airbox/z-padding convergence, and provenance as reusable artifacts for driven response and modal/eigenfrequency stages.

Supercell reports now also record and gate mesh-independent relaxation-state comparability. A repeated-unit initial-state writer and a headless `--initial-magnetization-state` runtime override with validator-enforced `initial_magnetization_state_override` provenance now exist for controlled/frozen supercell fixtures; the validator also verifies that the recorded JSON source state matches artifact `m_initial.json`, so metadata-only claims cannot pass. The repeated-state managed target now also writes central-cell extraction and a separate primitive-vs-repeated-state supercell report, and that report carries `supercell_initial_magnetization_state_override` so `FULLMAG_PBC_RELAX_REPEATED_STATE_SUPERCELL_REPORT` can be used as an explicit strict-gate input. `verify-fem-static-pbc-demag-equilibrium-repeated-state-runtime` is now the one-command wrapper that generates z-padding, an ordinary supercell diagnostic report, repeated-state supercell, and runs the strict CPU/GPU equilibrium gate with z-padding plus repeated-state report paths. Supercell prepare and repeated-state runs default `FULLMAG_PBC_RELAX_GMSH_THREADS=1` so the repeated-state file is not invalidated by multithreaded Gmsh node-count drift between two materializations. The repeated-state producer now fills air/non-magnetic nodes with `[0, 0, 0]`, matching native FEM `m` artifacts outside magnetic material, and the managed run passes the initial-state override validator. Supercell reports now include `mapped_central_cell_comparability`, a modulo-periodic nearest-node pointwise comparison for central-cell `m`, `H_demag`, and gauge-adjusted `demag_phi`; mapped pointwise errors, mapped nearest-node distances, and `same_local_discretization=true` are now report thresholds/contract, not only diagnostics. The comparator also has a dedicated `comparison_state="initial"` mode for operator-level checks on `m_initial` plus the first field snapshot, with final energy/torque thresholds deliberately omitted in that mode, and the native FEM runner now writes requested step-0 field snapshots before relaxation so that mode is backed by real runtime Zarr samples. Multi-sample field Zarr artifacts are now accepted by the runtime validator and comparator by selecting the sample row from `samples.csv`, and the same-local tiled supercell fixture now preserves `m_initial` plus every Zarr sample so the diagnostic target verifies both final and initial-state reports. `interpolated_remesh` is now a separate opt-in supercell acceptance basis: `verify-fem-static-pbc-demag-supercell-interpolated-artifacts` writes `acceptance_basis="interpolated_remesh"` reports from primitive tetrahedral interpolation, and `--require-interpolated-supercell-report` validates those reports without changing the default strict same-local `--require-supercell-report` contract. Repeated-state operator checks can now use asymmetric states: `--unit-state final --supercell-state initial` writes `comparison_state="final_to_initial"`, so the seeded supercell initial field is compared to the primitive final field that generated the seed, not to primitive `m_initial`. The strict same-local-discretization acceptance still fails on the current independent 3x3 remesh because nearest-node central-cell distances remain nanometre-scale. The controlled repeated-state seed path now uses interpolated remesh mapping and unit-normalizes the generated state, so the seeded `m` operator check passes on regenerated artifacts (`interpolated_m_p99_l2_delta=5.254146e-04`). The managed repeated-state wrapper now uses the explicit `interpolated_remesh` acceptance target for its final-state report instead of stopping on nearest-node remesh mismatch. The regenerated final-state report fails on `interpolated_h_demag_p99_relative_error=2.162412e-01`, `interpolated_demag_phi_max_abs_delta_after_offset_A=1.018032e-04`, and `e_demag_density_relative_error=2.404344e-02`, while interpolated `m` and central-cell torque pass. The regenerated `final_to_initial` operator report also fails before relaxation-level ambiguity on demag observables (`interpolated_h_demag_p99_relative_error=2.163426e-01`, `interpolated_demag_phi_max_abs_delta_after_offset_A=1.068777e-04`) while seeded `m` passes. A fresh 2026-07-01 managed independent 3x3 run after the Robin fix was stopped at step 530 because it still had `max_steps=4000` and oscillated near the torque threshold, so the next implementation step is a runtime-produced same-local supercell or fixing the demag/PBC operator mismatch exposed by the failing explicit `interpolated_remesh` reports, not treating independent 3x3 relaxation as a fast gate. M5 still blocks periodic-airbox frequency response/eigen promotion. |

Managed runtime gates pass for current single-sample/free, single-k frequency-window, lowest-branch reference nonzero-k Floquet k-path, gamma-only production CPU k-path adapter/provenance bundles, and the production CPU windowed nonzero-k Bloch/Floquet k-path gate with analytic exchange-only scale plus reciprocal `f(k)=f(-k)` evidence. The artifact validator keeps the nonzero-k production acceptance gate (`--require-production-modal-k-path`) separate from the gamma-only production bridge gate (`--require-production-gamma-k-path`), so a flat gamma bundle cannot close COMSOL-like nonzero-k dispersion. ## 5. Detailed Milestones

### M0 - Truth Consolidation And Live Plan Governance

Progress: 100%.

Delivered:

- Existing docs, active plans, physics notes, specs, reports, and COMSOL reference were located.
- Current backend/frontend implementation was audited from the working tree.
- This masterplan now acts as the progress ledger for ongoing rollout.

Exit gate:

- This file exists and is linked from the active masterplan README.
- Future implementation changes update this file before reporting milestone progress.

### M1 - Product Split And Semantic Spine

Progress: 78%.

Delivered:

- Frequency-domain family split is documented in physics notes and active plans.
- Python DSL exposes `Eigenmodes` and `FrequencyResponse`.
- ProblemIR and planner know the two study products.
- Control Room routes modal and driven resources separately.
- COMSOL parity language is aligned: `Eigenfrequency` maps to modal/eigen, `Frequency Domain` maps to driven harmonic response.

Remaining:

- Close audit P0: one phasor convention, one `L/B_alpha` or equivalent operator vocabulary, correct gyrotropic pencil, corrected dynamic-demag sign, `Ms`-correct observables, and damping/linewidth convention.
- Confirm all user-facing copy avoids collapsing modal and response products.
- Keep `frequency_domain_capabilities.v1` aligned with capability matrix rows.
- Add regression tests around any newly exposed UI calculation mode lowering.
- Keep dispersion UI summaries lane-aware: reference CPU modal k-path artifacts
  are visible, production selected-spectrum CPU remains gated, and modal GPU
  remains unavailable until a native modal GPU eigensolver exists.

Verification:

```bash
cargo test -p fullmag-ir frequency_response
cargo test -p fullmag-plan frequency_response
PYTHONPATH=packages/fullmag-py/src python3 -m pytest packages/fullmag-py/tests/test_api.py -k "frequency_response or eigenmodes"
python3 -m pytest scripts/test_frequency_domain_math_contracts.py -q
```

- Per-frequency point resources and Zarr-backed field payloads are represented.
- Progress, cancel, diagnostics, manifest, periodic-pair, and derived peak-mode validation is in place.

  legacy no-`Ms` proxy outputs and native `Ms`-correct SI susceptibility /

  `manifest.artifacts.response_progress_v1_path` and
  `manifest.resources.response_progress_resource_key`, matching the existing

  conventions.

- Solved periodic-airbox runs must publish the same complete artifact bundle as bounded failure runs.

### M4 - GPU Driven Response, Free And Static-Periodic No-Demag

Progress: 72%.

Delivered:

- CUDA tangent operator exists for exchange, local precession/mass, Zeeman, uniaxial anisotropy, and damping terms.
- Forced GPU does not silently fall back to CPU/dense validation.
- GPU free no-demag and static-periodic no-demag response runtime recipes exist.
- CPU/GPU shared-domain no-demag response compacts shared-airbox meshes to the magnetic-node slice instead of rejecting the mesh shape.
- GPU Floquet no-demag development smoke can carry phase projection metadata and response artifacts.

- Strict qualification of all included terms and precision modes.
- Explicit rejection artifacts for GPU periodic-airbox dynamic demag must remain stable.
- No-demag labels must stay visible in UI/provenance.

### M5 - Static PBC Equilibrium And Demag Stabilization

Progress: 73%.

Objective:

- Establish a physically accepted static state for magnonic-crystal unit cells before any harmonic response or eigenfrequency calculation. The reference target is a thin ferromagnetic film unit cell with a centered hole, lateral PBC, open z airbox, static bias field, and static demag enabled through the PBC-compatible airbox contract. The general static/time-domain PBC contract is axis-based, not plane-only: `z` may be periodic for non-fully-periodic cells when at least one other axis remains open and the mesh publishes matching `z_faces` periodic pairs.

Delivered:

- Static PBC demag physics and validity limits are documented in `docs/physics/0800-fem-static-pbc-demag.md`.
- The target antidot geometry and periodic-airbox constraints are tracked in `docs/plans/active/fullmag_pbc_fem_bloch_airbox_plan.md`.
- Frozen magnetic submesh and periodic pair preparation are scriptable through `scripts/prepare_fmr_frozen_magnetic_submesh.py`.
- Primitive-vs-supercell comparison has a validator in `scripts/verify_fem_frequency_domain_supercell_artifacts.py`.
- Existing static PBC demag evidence covers reduced periodic classes, periodic-pair diagnostics, open-axis Robin treatment, and primitive/supercell artifact comparison for static demag observables.
- Static/time-domain PBC is now explicit problem intent: `study.pbc(x=True, y=True, demag="periodic_airbox_k0")` lowers to `ProblemIR.pbc`, the planner rejects periodic FEM mesh pairs without `ProblemIR.pbc`, the planner rejects FEM static/time-domain demag PBC when `ProblemIR.pbc.demag` remains `open`, and runtime metadata publishes top-level `metadata.pbc` copied from the IR.
- Static/time-domain PBC axes are now explicit too: `study.pbc(z=True)` is legal public intent, derives `z_faces`, and the FEM planner rejects meshes whose inferred periodic axes add, remove, or replace the axes declared in `ProblemIR.pbc`. The current antidot examples intentionally use lateral `x/y` PBC with open `z`; this does not limit future supported `z`-periodic non-full-3D cases.
- A planner regression now proves the positive `z` demag case: single-axis `z` FEM demag PBC with open `x/y` airbox boundaries and `ProblemIR.pbc.demag = "periodic_airbox_k0"` plans, while the existing full `x/y/z` demag rejection remains in force for the current non-full-3D airbox slice.
- `examples/fem_periodic_uniform_slab_relax_exchange_coupled.py` and `just verify-fem-static-pbc-demag-uniform-slab-runtime` now provide the minimal false-PBC managed diagnostic before the hole geometry: a uniform exchange-coupled `200 nm x 200 nm x 10 nm` slab with `x/y` `periodic_airbox_k0`, open `z`, a short explicit `max_steps=120` relaxation, CPU validation, and GPU/device-Poisson validation.
- `just verify-fem-static-pbc-demag-equilibrium-repeated-state-runtime` now starts with `just verify-fem-static-pbc-demag-uniform-slab-runtime`, so the one-command strict-M5 wrapper exercises the simple false-PBC slab before it generates or validates any antidot z-padding/supercell reports.
- The periodic-pairs artifact writer and validator now require every selected periodic pair to prove shared-airbox node coverage plus non-empty boundary-face pair diagnostics with opposed normals. If the magnetic body crosses a selected seam, as in `exchange_coupled`, the pair must also prove positive magnetic coverage. If the magnetic body is a separated island inside an air gap, as in `air_gap`, `magnetic = 0` is accepted only when the same-step `phi`/`H_demag`, normal `B` flux, and side-charge diagnostics prove magnetostatic continuity.
- Periodic-antidot relaxation examples now request `H_demag`, `H_eff`, and `demag_phi` snapshots, and runtime validators require `metadata.pbc`, the final equilibrium field `m_final.json`, scalar history `scalars.csv` with non-increasing final total energy, the resolved same-step `fields/H_demag/step_*.json`, resolved same-step `fields/demag_phi/step_*.json`, and `mesh/periodic_pairs.v1.json` diagnostic artifacts with explicit `node_pairs`, converged demag Poisson telemetry in `demag_runtime`, PBC pair topology, finite final torque, non-negative final demag energy, magnetic-body norm preservation, final `m`/`H_demag` seam mismatch below tolerance, `demag_phi` seam mismatch below tolerance after removing the best constant offset per periodic pair id, and GPU device-Poisson provenance for the GPU gate.
- The periodic-antidot validator now requires same-step `diagnostics/fem_static_pbc_demag_seams.v1.json` with normal `B` flux and side magnetic charge diagnostics, so a run cannot pass by showing periodic `m` while preserving finite-cell magnetostatic seams.
- The runner now emits the same-step static PBC demag seam diagnostics artifact for FEM static/time-domain `periodic_airbox_k0` demag runs when final `H_demag` and `demag_phi` snapshots are present; incomplete data produces a `status="failed"` artifact rather than silent acceptance.
- The managed periodic-antidot CPU/GPU runtime recipes now pass optional strict M5 comparison reports into the validator through `FULLMAG_PBC_RELAX_Z_PADDING_REPORT` and `FULLMAG_PBC_RELAX_SUPERCELL_REPORT`; when set, the validator requires `fem_static_pbc_z_padding_validation.v1` and `fem_static_pbc_supercell_validation.v1` reports with `status="ok"` plus finite, non-negative, below-threshold static-demag comparison metrics. The z-padding report uses demag-energy relative error, `p99(|H_demag|)` relative error, and `demag_phi` range relative error as acceptance metrics; global `|H_demag|` maximum and absolute `demag_phi` deltas remain diagnostics.
- Strict z-padding reports now prove z-padding geometry, not just two compatible artifact roots: the report writer and runtime validator require an `x/y` periodic, open-`z` workload with matching lateral universe size and a strictly thicker reference open-`z` airbox than the candidate. Same-airbox CPU/GPU ordinary relaxation artifacts cannot masquerade as z-padding convergence evidence. A fresh managed exchange-coupled z-padding run now writes `status="ok"` at `.fullmag/reports/fem-static-pbc-demag-equilibrium-runtime/reports/z_padding_validation.v1.json`; strict M5 remains open until the matching primitive-vs-supercell report and full equilibrium gate pass.
- `examples/fem_periodic_antidot_relax_exchange_coupled_z_padding_reference.py` and `just verify-fem-static-pbc-demag-z-padding-runtime` now provide the canonical managed path to produce the exchange-coupled z-padding report from a 90 nm open-`z` candidate and a 130 nm open-`z` reference for the same workload.
- Strict supercell reports now prove repeated-cell geometry too: the report writer and runtime validator require lateral `universe_size_m` scaled by `repeat_x/repeat_y`, matching open-`z` `universe_size_m`, and matching lateral air gap, periodic pair ids, and exchange-coupling intent, so a same-size ordinary artifact root cannot masquerade as primitive-vs-supercell validation.
- `examples/fem_periodic_antidot_relax_exchange_coupled_supercell_3x3.py` and `just verify-fem-static-pbc-demag-supercell-runtime` now provide the canonical managed path to produce primitive/3x3 supercell artifacts for the exchange-coupled workload and write the strict supercell report from automatically extracted central-cell indices and scalars. `just prepare-fem-static-pbc-demag-supercell-runtime-artifacts` can now produce and ordinary-validate the unit/supercell runtime artifact roots before strict report generation; repeated artifacts are accepted only through explicit `--supercell-repeat 3 3` validation and scaled lateral `universe_size_m`, and repeated FEM artifact roots now include `mesh/node_geometry.v1.json` so central-cell index selection can be audited against node coordinates and magnetic-node masks.
- `scripts/write_fem_static_pbc_supercell_central_cell_artifact.py` and `just write-fem-static-pbc-demag-supercell-central-cell-artifact-auto` now provide the default central-cell extraction artifact producer from `mesh/node_geometry.v1.json`, `metadata.execution_plan.backend_plan.mesh`, `m_final.json`, `H_demag.zarr`, and `H_eff.zarr`; the producer selects magnetic-node and node-aligned field indices from the central repeated cell, computes central-cell demag energy by element-centroid magnetic tetrahedron integration, computes central-cell torque residual from `max(norm(cross(m_i,H_eff_i)))`, validates index ranges against `m_final.json`, `H_demag.zarr`, `H_eff.zarr`, and `demag_phi.zarr`, rejects central scalar values above global supercell `E_demag` or final torque, and does not infer central energy or torque from global supercell totals. The manual target still accepts explicit index lists or index files plus explicit scalar values for external extraction workflows.
- The repeated-state runtime now writes `supercell_interpolated_initial_operator_validation.v1.json` through `write-fem-static-pbc-demag-supercell-repeated-state-initial-operator-diagnostic-report` before attempting the strict final-state interpolated report. This preserves the `final_to_initial` operator-level demag/PBC mismatch report even when final strict acceptance is expected to fail.
- `scripts/compare_fem_static_pbc_equilibrium_artifacts.py` now writes those strict static comparison reports from real artifact roots, rejects self-comparison and incompatible-workload roots, records the compared workload identity, requires `diagnostics/fem_static_pbc_supercell_central_cell.v1.json` for primitive-vs-supercell comparisons, the periodic-antidot validator rejects missing or mismatched report workload metadata, missing central-cell extraction summaries, or over-threshold report metrics, and `just verify-fem-static-pbc-demag-equilibrium-runtime` requires the two report paths before running the ordinary CPU/GPU periodic-antidot gates.
- GPU projected-gradient BB and nonlinear-CG now project every trial magnetisation onto the static periodic representative map after nodal-sphere retraction, so the demag RHS does not see a synthetic seam in `m` during line-search energy evaluation.
- GPU device Poisson demag recovery now projects recovered `H_demag` onto static periodic classes before energy and snapshot diagnostics. This fixes the observed false-PBC state where the GPU run had periodic `m` but a non-periodic `H_demag` seam.
- Fresh managed CPU and GPU periodic-antidot relaxation runs pass the ordinary validator for both `exchange_coupled` and `air_gap`. Latest artifact evidence:
  - CPU `exchange_coupled`: 212 steps, final torque `4630.688509775621 A/m`, `E_total=-2.291265785283922e-18 J`, `max_H_seam=0`, `max_Bn_seam=0`.
  - CPU `air_gap`: 809 steps, final torque `4991.247845003653 A/m`, `E_total=4.8840367289235546e-18 J`, `max_H_seam=0`, `max_Bn_seam=0`, airbox-only side seams with `magnetic=0`.
  - GPU `exchange_coupled`: 179 steps, final torque `4128.4717381323535 A/m`, `E_total=-2.2925217615732664e-18 J`, `max_H_seam=0`, `max_Bn_seam=0`, `demag_operator_mode="device_hypre_poisson"`.
  - GPU `air_gap`: 1183 steps, final torque `4987.821957028258 A/m`, `E_total=4.889581843952544e-18 J`, `max_H_seam=0`, `max_Bn_seam=0`, airbox-only side seams with `magnetic=0`, `demag_operator_mode="device_hypre_poisson"`.

Acceptance gates:

1. The authored problem declares the selected static PBC axes through `ProblemIR.pbc` with `demag = "periodic_airbox_k0"`, the inferred mesh periodic axes match `ProblemIR.pbc.axes`, and the mesh publishes complete shared-airbox `periodic_node_pairs`, magnetic `periodic_node_pairs` for seams where magnetic material crosses the periodic boundary, periodic boundary-face pair translations/orientation diagnostics, material labels, and airbox/open-axis boundary labels. The current antidot acceptance fixture is `x/y` periodic with open `z`; that fixture must not be treated as a solver-wide ban on `z`-periodic non-full-3D cells.
2. The resolved magnetostatic model is `periodic_airbox_k0` or the static PBC demag equivalent; the run must not fall back to finite isolated-airbox demag or accept `ProblemIR.pbc.demag = "open"` for FEM static/time-domain demag PBC.
3. Relaxation reaches a physical equilibrium by torque residual and stable energy behavior, not merely by a low `max_steps` smoke.
4. Magnetization keeps unit length in the magnetic body and has seam mismatch below the documented PBC tolerance.
5. Static `H_demag`, scalar potential, demag energy, and normal `B` flux satisfy seam, sign, gauge, and z-padding convergence checks; `demag_phi` seam diagnostics must remove the best constant offset per periodic pair id before applying the tolerance, and side periodic seams must not exhibit artificial magnetic surface charges.
6. The primitive PBC cell agrees with an explicit 2x2 or 3x3 supercell central-cell extraction for average magnetization, demag energy density, `H_demag` statistics, flux/seam diagnostics, and torque residual.
7. Accepted artifacts include `metadata.pbc`, the equilibrium field, demag diagnostics, periodic-pair summary, z-padding/supercell comparison, solver convergence, and requested/resolved provenance.
8. Control Room can inspect the accepted equilibrium and show PBC/demag diagnostics before the user launches response or eigenfrequency analysis.

just verify-fem-static-pbc-demag-supercell-runtime

### M8 - COMSOL-Class Modal/Eigenfrequency Solver

Progress: 38%.

Delivered:

- Modal/eigen product is semantically separate from driven response.
- Native request/result contracts and SLEPc-facing adapter pieces exist.

  `Eigenmodes.count`, and the artifact validator rejects window bundles where
  published `mode_count` exceeds that requested cap.
- Runner-side production modal dense solves now pass `B=-G` as the generalized
  pencil RHS for the canonical `exp(i omega t)` / `lambda=i omega` contract,
  and operator diagnostics name `pencil_B=-G`.
- Runner-side native modal result ingestion now rejects inconsistent
  `lambda=i omega` frequency mapping: accepted modes must have positive
  `Im(lambda)`, `omega_rad_s=Im(lambda)`, and
  `frequency_hz=Im(lambda)/(2*pi)`.
- A focused one-node macrospin residual regression now checks
  `K phi = lambda B phi` with `B=-G` and `lambda=i`, so the guard covers
  sign, branch, frequency magnitude, and residual mapping for the local
  pencil boundary.
- The eigen artifact validator now rejects `lambda_eq_i_omega` modal bundles
  whose mode summary, per-mode metadata, or eigen-summary entry has a
  non-positive `Im(lambda)` or inconsistent `omega_rad_s` /
  `frequency_hz` mapping.
- The physics note, artifact spec, and math-contract doc test now distinguish
  physical `G` from the solver RHS pencil matrix for the energy-gyrotropic
  formulation.

Remaining:

- Finish the modal algebra proof before expanding SLEPc beyond the current
  dense-adapter gates: runner-side local guards and artifact validation now
  cover `B=-G`, branch, frequency mapping, and macrospin residual, but
  production modal promotion still needs end-to-end native/SLEPc
  eigenvalue-to-frequency residual proof.
- Extend the modal proof beyond local undamped macrospin checks to damped
  complex-frequency/linewidth artifacts, normalization/orthogonality, and
  selected-spectrum residual evidence.
- Implement scalable selected-spectrum modal eigensolver for large FEM meshes.
- Finish production selected-spectrum solving for frequency windows with
  `frequency_min_hz`, `frequency_max_hz`, and `count` as maximum accepted modes.
- Emit residuals, mass norm, orthogonality, tangent leakage, solver diagnostics, spectral transform, KSP/PC provenance, and stop reason.
- Connect modal native execution to runner and managed runtime proof.

Progress: 88%.

Delivered:

- Control Room now preserves the `label` column from `dispersion.csv` as a

  labels such as `G sample 0, mode 1` in point controls, and Analysis Plots
  shows the same label in selected-point summaries instead of reducing every
  selected k-path point to a raw numeric `path_s` value.
- Control Room now preserves the `line_width_hz` column from `dispersion.csv`
  as per-point modal `linewidthHz`, so damping/linewidth evidence hardened in
  the backend artifact validator remains available to frontend chart and
  inspection consumers. Analysis Plots also carries that value through chart
  cursor points and shows linewidth in selected dispersion-point summaries.
- The frequency-domain manifest and generated OpenAPI now expose modal
  dispersion lane status as `dispersion.reference_cpu`,
  `dispersion.production_cpu`, `dispersion.production_cpu_gamma_k_path`, and
  `dispersion.production_gpu`. Control Room diagnostics and dispersion
  summaries show those lane statuses alongside `k_path` and `branch_tracking`,
  preventing the current CPU/reference k-path artifact lane or the gamma-only
  production CPU provenance bridge from being mistaken for nonzero-k production
  CPU/GPU modal dispersion.
- Control Room solver diagnostics now also display the artifact-level
  `production_cpu_rejection_reason` and `production_cpu_rejection_scope` for
  reference nonzero-k Floquet modal bundles, so the selected-spectrum
  production gap is visible at the same inspection surface as modal transport
  and residual diagnostics.
- `eigen/diagnostics/solver.v1.json` for modal periodic/Floquet paths now
  publishes `basis_transport_policy`,
  `floquet_tangent_frame_max_mismatch`, and
  `floquet_tangent_transport_max_nonunitarity`; the M9 managed k-path validator
  requires `basis_transport_policy="tangent_frame_transport"` for the current
  CPU/reference `Full2x2` Floquet gate.
- The current runtime writer in `crates/fullmag-runner/src/fem_eigen.rs` now
  emits the same seed-only tracking provenance and mode-field handoff fields for
  single-sample/free and frequency-window modal bundles, so the hardened
  validator also protects `just verify-fem-frequency-domain-eigen-runtime`.
- `just verify-fem-frequency-domain-eigen-dispersion-runtime` now runs a small
  Python DSL Floquet k-path example with `study.pbc(x=True)`,
  `fm.FloquetBC(["x_faces"])`, `operator="full_2x2"`,
  reciprocal `KPath(G-X-G--X)`, `target="lowest"`, `save("dispersion")`, and
  requested `mode` output through the managed FEM runtime, then validates
  `eigen/spectrum.v2.json`, `eigen/branches.v2.json`,
  `eigen/dispersion.csv`, per-sample mode metadata, Zarr mode payloads,
  `eigen/metadata/eigen_summary.json`,
  `eigen/diagnostics/solver.v1.json`, and
  `frequency_domain/manifest.v1.json`. The managed artifact validator now
  requires `solver_model="reference_full_2x2_tangent"` and per-mode
  `solver_model="cpu_full_2x2_phase_reduced_floquet"` for this gate, plus
  exchange-only analytic scale and `f(k)=f(-k)` reciprocal checks, so scalar,
  gamma-only, wrong-scale, or nonreciprocal fallback cannot pass the M9
  dispersion runtime target.
- Reference/MVP k-path `FrequencyWindow` requests now publish
  `requested_window_hz`, `resolved_search_window_hz`,
  `window_completeness.status="not_certified"`, `subwindows`, and
  `frequency_window_solver_policy` in `eigen/diagnostics/solver.v1.json` and
  `eigen/metadata/eigen_summary.json`. The validator now rejects the managed
  reference `Full2x2` Floquet k-path window gate if that policy is missing or
  if the artifact implies production shift-invert semantics instead of
  `reference_k_path_window_filter_not_shift_invert_or_feast`. Branch tracking
  also skips empty
  intermediate k-samples without panicking, so a frequency-window filter cannot
  crash the k-path artifact writer when one sample has no accepted modes.
- `scripts/verify_fem_frequency_domain_eigen_artifacts.py` now exposes
  `--require-production-modal-k-path` as a separate production acceptance gate
  for COMSOL-like nonzero-k modal dispersion. The flag requires multi-sample
  selected-spectrum provenance from the managed production modal adapter
  (`spectral_transform="shift_invert"`, `execution_lane="production_cpu"`,
  `production_solver_available=true`, `dense_reference_oracle=false`), at least
  one nonzero sampled `k_vector`, and rejects the current reference
  `reference_k_path_window_filter_not_shift_invert_or_feast` policy. This is a
  gate for future production CPU evidence, not a claim that the current k-path
  runner has been promoted.
- The managed gamma-equivalent production CPU k-path bridge now uses a separate
  `--require-production-gamma-k-path` validator flag. It requires the same
  production selected-spectrum provenance but rejects any nonzero sampled
  `k_vector`, so this adapter proof cannot satisfy the future nonzero-k
  production modal dispersion gate.
- Both production modal k-path gates now enforce the current no-demag/no-DMI
  scope at the manifest boundary: `requested_execution.include_demag=false`,
  `resolved_execution.demag_realization="none"`, and no gated demag, dynamic
  demag, periodic-Poisson, Floquet-airbox demag, DMI, or magnetoelastic entries
  in `operator_terms_included[]`. This prevents M9 production CPU no-demag
  evidence from being reused as dynamic-demag or DMI production proof.
- The k-path orchestrator now has an explicit production CPU shift-invert
  solver model token and rejects mixed per-sample solver models, so a future
  production selected-spectrum k-path cannot silently aggregate one production
  sample with reference samples and report only the first sample's provenance.
  The dispatch bridge also preserves production CPU shift-invert provenance
  from a single-k `eigen_summary` when that per-sample solve eventually becomes
  eligible.
- K-path `eigen/diagnostics/solver.v1.json` generation is now
  production-aware: if every sample resolves to the production CPU
  shift-invert model, the aggregate diagnostics report
  `resolved_solver_family="shift_invert"`, `spectral_transform="shift_invert"`,
  `solver_adapter="slepc_modal_eigen"`, `execution_lane="production_cpu"`,
  `phasor_convention="exp_i_omega_t"`, and omit the reference-only
  `reference_k_path_window_filter_not_shift_invert_or_feast` policy. The
  matching frequency-domain manifest now labels the aggregate path as
  production/native CPU and uses the same `exp_i_omega_t` convention.
- Single-k gamma frequency-window execution now treats explicit
  `KSamplingIR::Single { k_vector: [0,0,0] }` as equivalent to omitted
  `k_sampling` for the native CPU `Full2x2` shift-invert window gate, and the
  native modal request forwards that explicit k-vector through the ABI instead
  of dropping it. This gamma-equivalent bridge remains separate from the
  nonzero-k Bloch/Floquet production slice below.
- The managed windowed `Full2x2` no-demag Bloch/Floquet k-path target now runs
  through the production CPU selected-spectrum lane and is validated with
  `--require-production-modal-k-path`, analytic exchange-only scale checks, and
  reciprocal `f(k)=f(-k)` checks. This promotes the CPU no-demag selected-
  spectrum k-path slice only; it does not promote dynamic demag-k, GPU modal
  dispersion, DMI, periodic Poisson, or general magnonic-crystal FMR.
- M9 production acceptance still needs DE and BV low-k fixtures: `|k| <=
  2e6..3e6 rad/m` (`2..3 1/um`), frequency/modal window up to about `5 GHz`,
  and analytic comparison for the chosen film geometry/material. The current
  exchange-only higher-k checks remain unit/sign/order-of-magnitude stress
  tests, not the final scientific acceptance shape.
- The artifact validator now has the first DE/BV low-k acceptance hook:
  `--require-low-k-de-bv-analytic-dispersion` requires
  `metadata.json.execution_plan.backend_plan.dispersion_validation` to declare
  `kind="thin_film_de_bv_low_k"`, `analytic_model="kalinikos_slab_n0"`, film
  thickness, equilibrium magnetization, film normal, `frequency_window_hz`,
  `max_k_rad_per_m <= 3e6`, and both Damon-Eshbach and backward-volume scenario
  entries with `branch_id` plus `sample_indices`. The validator checks the
  low-k range, in-plane k, DE/BV orientation, window not exceeding 5 GHz, and
  branch frequency agreement with the analytic slab formula.
- FEM eigen planning now has the runtime metadata handoff for that hook:
  authored `problem_meta.runtime_metadata.dispersion_validation` is copied into
  `execution_plan.backend_plan.dispersion_validation`, so runtime artifact
  validation can check the same DE/BV acceptance intent that was attached to the
  problem. Python authoring now has `fm.DispersionValidationScenario`,
  `fm.ThinFilmDEBVDispersionValidation`, `fm.dispersion_validation(...)`, and
  `study.dispersion_validation(...)`, so scripts can attach the DE/BV low-k
  validation intent without hand-writing backend-plan metadata. The planner now
  lowers this into typed `FemEigenDispersionValidationIR` and rejects invalid
  validation shape, broad k ranges, missing DE/BV scenarios, invalid vectors, or
  frequency windows above 5 GHz before runtime artifacts are written. The first
  positive DE/BV gate now exists as a CPU/reference analytic Kalinikos `n=0`
  adapter: it samples the authored BV and DE k-path, writes modal dispersion
  artifacts, and validates them with
  `--require-low-k-de-bv-analytic-dispersion`. This is a reference acceptance
  slice for the analytical curve and artifact contract; it is not the full
  phase-aware FEM dynamic-demag-k operator and not a production CPU/GPU modal
  promotion.
- Runtime modal k-path manifests now have a resource-visible handoff for that
  same validation intent:
  `frequency_domain/manifest.v1.json.validation.dispersion_validation` mirrors
  the typed FEM eigen plan payload, so the
  existing frequency-domain manifest resource can show whether the run was set
  up for DE/BV low-k analytic acceptance. This remains provenance and test
  intent; it does not promote the current no-demag exchange-only k-path gate to
  DE/BV dynamic-demag acceptance.
- `examples/fem_eigenmodes_dispersion_de_bv_low_k.py` is the canonical target
  input for the real DE/BV low-k acceptance gate: it declares `x/y` PBC,
  `FloquetBC(["x_faces", "y_faces"])`, `include_demag=True`, `|k| <= 3e6
  rad/m`, `0..5 GHz`, and `ThinFilmDEBVDispersionValidation`. It is now wired
  into `verify-fem-frequency-domain-eigen-dispersion-de-bv-low-k-runtime`,
  which passes only through the CPU/reference analytic
  `reference_thin_film_de_bv_kalinikos_n0` solver lane. M10 remains open for
  the real phase-aware FEM dynamic-demag-k operator and for any production
  CPU/GPU modal promotion.
- Production modal/gamma k-path window artifacts remain explicitly
  `window_completeness.status="not_certified"` with
  `certification_method="none"` and `additional_modes_may_exist=true` until a
  later sparse/matrix-free or contour/count validation gate proves certified
  modal counts for the selected window.
- Production modal/gamma k-path mode summaries now have explicit quality
  guards: `residual_relative_l2 <= 1e-6` and
  `tangent_leakage_max_abs <= 1e-8`. This guards the current no-demag
  selected-spectrum artifact slice against numerically bad published modes
  without claiming certified window completeness or dynamic-demag production.
- K-path `eigen/dispersion.csv` publication now maps positive modal
  `frequency_imag_hz` to the public FWHM `line_width_hz = 2 *
  frequency_imag_hz`, matching the single-sample writer and the artifact
  validator. This keeps damped modal dispersion rows from silently dropping
  linewidth evidence in the public CSV.
- The nonzero-k production modal k-path gate now requires vector-aware branch
  tracking evidence: `branches.v2.json` summary and diagnostics plus
  `frequency_domain/manifest.v1.json.diagnostics` must report
  `modal_overlap_available=true` and
  `tracking_score_source="modal_overlap_weighted_score"`; mixed fallback
  summary sources are not production acceptance evidence. `branches.v2.json`
  must report `tracking_method="overlap_hungarian"`, no branch point may use
  `frequency_score_fallback`, and at least one branch point must publish
  `tracking_score_source="modal_overlap_weighted_score"`. `overlap_floor`,
  `diagnostics.min_overlap`, and non-seed `overlap_prev` /
  `tracking_confidence` values are enforced as finite `[0,1]` values, accepted
  modal-overlap summaries and points must not carry a stale
  `modal_overlap_unavailable_reason`, modal-overlap branch points must publish
  `overlap_prev`, cannot fall below the declared floor, and their
  `tracking_confidence` must match `overlap_prev`; `diagnostics.min_overlap`
  must also match the minimum modal-overlap `overlap_prev` value in the branch
  points, and `diagnostics.median_overlap` must publish the corresponding
  median.
  `eigen/dispersion.csv.overlap_score` must also match the corresponding branch
  point `overlap_prev`, so chart/UI handoff cannot report a different tracking
  quality than `branches.v2.json`. The
  gamma-only production bridge remains separate and cannot close this nonzero-k
  modal-overlap proof.
- The nonzero-k production modal k-path gate now also requires
  `basis_transport_policy="tangent_frame_transport"` plus finite
  `floquet_tangent_frame_max_mismatch` and
  `floquet_tangent_transport_max_nonunitarity`, keeping the gamma-only
  production bridge separate from Floquet transport proof.
- Reference/MVP modal PBC/Floquet scalar-phase reduction now rejects raw
  equilibrium states whose selected periodic pairs have nonidentity tangent
  frames. This keeps `q_dst = phase*q_src` legal only for identity-frame pairs
  and reports the missing `phase*(T_dst^T T_src)` support with
  `tangent_frame_mismatch` diagnostics instead of silently applying the wrong
  modal constraint.
- Reference/MVP modal Floquet reduction now executes `operator="full_2x2"`
  for nonzero-k single-k and k-path requests by applying each selected-node
  Bloch phase together with the local tangent transport `T_node^T T_root`,
  i.e. reduced blocks are assembled as `R_i^H K_ij R_j` and `R_i^H M_ij R_j`
  with `R = phase * (T_node^T T_root)`. The path reports
  `reference_full_2x2_tangent` / `cpu_full_2x2_phase_reduced_floquet` metadata
  and reconstructs two complex tangent components per reduced node into
  complex 3D mode-field artifacts.
- Runner rejects forced GPU modal `KSamplingIR::Path` requests explicitly before backend execution, so driven-response GPU smoke or accidental single-k GPU solves cannot be reported as modal dispersion proof.
- Runner can forward a narrow explicit-GPU no-demag/no-DMI Floquet response slice to native code.
- GPU development smoke emits phase-projection metadata and no-demag artifacts.
- Capability matrix describes the slice as narrow/development, not full production.

Remaining:

- Broaden the production CPU no-demag k-path proof beyond the current small
  managed `Full2x2` exchange-only fixture: production native branch tracking
  with modal-overlap or a stronger vector-aware metric, residual/tangent
  leakage diagnostics audited directly from the production backend,
  convergence/window-completeness validation, and larger sparse/matrix-free
  scaling evidence remain required before calling it COMSOL-class large-object
  dispersion.
- Keep production modal GPU dispersion unavailable until a native modal GPU eigensolver and Floquet operator exist; do not reuse the driven-response GPU smoke as modal proof.
- Promote full tangent-frame transport beyond the current CPU `Full2x2` slice.
  Scalar Floquet reduction still rejects nonidentity paired tangent frames, and
  production GPU modal paths still need their own `phase*(T_dst^T T_src)`
  transport proof.
- Promote only after production validation for exchange graph assembly, reciprocal dispersion/response behavior, pair coverage, and CPU/GPU parity where applicable.
- Keep full periodic exchange-graph assembly, DMI, demag, periodic Poisson, and magnetoelastic response gated until implemented.
- Do not market this as general magnonic-crystal FMR.

Verification:

```bash
just verify-fem-frequency-domain-cpu-floquet-runtime
just verify-fem-frequency-domain-gpu-floquet-runtime
just verify-fem-frequency-domain-gpu-floquet-reciprocal-runtime
```
