## 6. Promotion Rules

A lane moves from `semantic_only` or `reference_executable` to `partial_production_executable` only when all of these are true:

1. Physics note names equations, units, assumptions, boundary conditions, observables, and validation limits.
2. Python DSL and ProblemIR can express the requested intent.
3. Planner accepts exactly the supported slice and rejects nearby unsupported slices.
4. Runner preserves requested and resolved execution.
5. Native backend executes through `backends/fem`, not hidden runner-only numerics.
6. Artifacts include manifest, diagnostics, provenance, response/eigen payloads, and field resources.
7. API facade and resource hooks can read the artifacts.
8. Control Room displays the result and capability status without misleading labels.
9. Managed/container-backed `just` verification passes for native FEM behavior.
10. Capability matrix and this masterplan are updated in the same change.

A lane moves to `validated` only after it also has analytical or cross-solver validation, convergence checks, CPU/GPU parity where relevant, and benchmark evidence for the named geometry/workload.

## 7. Current Highest-Priority Work

1. Close audit P0 mathematical contract in docs and tests before any new production promotion: phasor convention, gyrotropic pencil, demag sign, observable units, damping convention, and tangent-frame transport.
2. Close M5 static PBC equilibrium and demag stabilization for the antidot/magnonic-crystal unit cell, starting from a uniform periodic slab false-PBC test before the hole geometry.
3. After M5 passes and audit P0 is implemented, close M6 solved single-point and refined-spectrum acceptance for CPU `periodic_airbox_k0` driven response with demag.
4. Keep M4 GPU no-demag/static-periodic slices strict and parity-tested.
5. Finish M7 browser proof for complex eigen/response 3D overlays and transaction-backed stage authoring.
6. Advance M8 native modal/eigenfrequency selected-spectrum path only after the runner/artifact `B=-G` / positive-branch / frequency-mapping guards are extended to end-to-end native/SLEPc residual, damping, linewidth, normalization, and scalability tests.
7. Keep M10 nonzero-k dynamic demag explicitly gated until the real phase-aware demag operator exists.

## 7.1 Handoff - Dispersion Work Stopped On 2026-07-02

This section records the exact stop point for the dispersion implementation
thread. Work is intentionally paused here; do not infer completion from the
existence of `examples/dyspersje.png` or from the CPU/reference DE/BV analytic
bundle.

### Current readiness estimate

| Area | Readiness | Meaning |
|---|---:|---|
| Reference/modal k-path artifact plumbing | 90% | Public `Eigenmodes` k-path artifacts, branch tracking, path metadata, mode-field handoff, and validators are largely in place. |
| Realistic low-k DE/BV acceptance shape | 80% | The test policy, Python authoring intent, planner metadata, validator hook, and CPU/reference analytic adapter exist. |
| Production CPU no-demag modal k-path | 65% | The selected-spectrum CPU lane can publish no-demag Bloch/Floquet k-path artifacts and pass the current production no-demag gate, but window completeness and broader sparse/matrix-free validation are not certified. |
| Production CPU DE/BV with dynamic demag-k | 20% | The current passing DE/BV bundle is an analytic-reference adapter, not a FEM dynamic demag-k eigensolve. |
| Production GPU modal dispersion | 0-5% | Forced GPU modal k-path requests are rejected explicitly; no native GPU modal eigensolver/Floquet operator is implemented. |
| Release-ready COMSOL-like dispersion | 45-50% | We can calculate and plot a narrow DE/BV analytic/reference dispersion now, but production physical dispersion with dynamic demag-k and CPU/GPU validation is still open. |

### Detailed progress ledger at pause

| Layer / artifact | Done in this dispersion thread | Evidence now expected in the tree | Remaining work | Readiness |
|---|---|---|---|---:|

| Scientific test shape | Default dispersion acceptance is now narrow DE/BV low-k, not all-direction scans: DE has in-plane `k perpendicular m0`, BV has in-plane `k parallel m0`, `|k| <= 2e6..3e6 rad/m`, and a low-GHz window such as `0..5 GHz`. | This masterplan and the frequency-domain physics note both state the DE/BV matrix and analytic-comparison rule. | Add production numerical convergence evidence for the same fixture after dynamic demag-k exists. | 80% |

### What is already done

1. The documentation now states that the default scientific dispersion test is
   not an all-direction k-space sweep. The normal acceptance fixture is a
   narrow Damon-Eshbach plus backward-volume matrix:
   `|k| <= 2e6..3e6 rad/m` (`2..3 1/um`), a low-GHz window such as
   `0..5 GHz`, in-plane `k`, DE with `k perpendicular m0`, BV with
   `k parallel m0`, and comparison with declared thin-film analytic
   dispersion.
2. `examples/fem_eigenmodes_dispersion_de_bv_low_k.py` is the canonical
   DE/BV input for this acceptance shape. It declares `x/y` Floquet/PBC,
   `include_demag=True`, `0..5 GHz`, `|k| <= 3e6 rad/m`, and
   `ThinFilmDEBVDispersionValidation`.
3. Public Python authoring exists for the validation intent:
   `DispersionValidationScenario`, `ThinFilmDEBVDispersionValidation`,
   `fm.dispersion_validation(...)`, and `study.dispersion_validation(...)`.
4. The FEM planner carries typed `FemEigenDispersionValidationIR` instead of
   loose JSON. It validates `kind="thin_film_de_bv_low_k"`,
   `analytic_model="kalinikos_slab_n0"`, valid film normal, in-plane
   magnetization, positive thickness, `max_k_rad_per_m <= 3e6`, valid
   `0..5 GHz` window, and both DE/BV scenarios before writing the execution
   plan.
5. Runtime metadata and modal artifact manifests now carry the DE/BV validation
   intent. `frequency_domain/manifest.v1.json.validation.dispersion_validation`
   is required to match
   `metadata.json.execution_plan.backend_plan.dispersion_validation`.
6. `eigen/dispersion.csv` carries the analytic comparison columns for accepted
   DE/BV samples: `analytic_frequency_hz`, `relative_error`, and
   `validation_geometry`.
7. `scripts/verify_fem_frequency_domain_eigen_artifacts.py` has
   `--require-low-k-de-bv-analytic-dispersion`. It checks both DE and BV,
   in-plane orientation, `|k| <= 3e6 rad/m`, frequency window not above
   `5 GHz`, branch/sample mapping, and relative error against
   `kalinikos_slab_n0`.
8. `scripts/plot_fem_frequency_domain_eigen_artifacts.py` can generate
   `examples/dyspersje.png` from the DE/BV artifact bundle with numerical
   branch and analytic reference overlay on the same k-path axis.
9. The current CPU/reference DE/BV managed bundle can produce a plot with
   branch frequencies around `2.958..3.106 GHz` and zero analytic relative
   error because the frequency source is the analytic Kalinikos `n=0` adapter.
10. The latest artifact/manifest contract change distinguishes the source of
    the plotted dispersion:
    `dispersion_frequency_source="analytic_reference_model"` for the current
    CPU/reference DE/BV adapter, and
    `dispersion_frequency_source="numeric_modal_solver_with_analytic_comparison"`
    for a future numerical modal solver that is compared to analytic DE/BV.
    The manifest also records
    `dynamic_demag_operator_source="analytic_thin_film_de_bv_reference_not_fem_demag_k"`
    for the current adapter, so it cannot be misread as implemented FEM
    dynamic demag-k.
11. `PathSolveResult.include_demag` now feeds
    `frequency_domain/manifest.v1.json.requested_execution.include_demag` for
    eigen bundles, so DE/BV examples that request demag preserve that requested
    intent even when the current solver lane is an analytic-reference adapter.

CARGO_TARGET_DIR=.fullmag/cargo-target CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 RUSTFLAGS='-Cdebuginfo=0' CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=1 cargo test -p fullmag-runner de_bv_reference_manifest_names_analytic_frequency_source_not_demag_k --lib
CARGO_TARGET_DIR=.fullmag/cargo-target CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 RUSTFLAGS='-Cdebuginfo=0' CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=1 cargo test -p fullmag-runner production_dispersion_with_de_bv_validation_writes_analytic_columns --lib

The container-backed managed proof for the newest manifest-source fields now
passes after fixing the manual k-path manifest writer in
`crates/fullmag-runner/src/dispatch.rs`:

```bash
just verify-fem-frequency-domain-eigen-dispersion-de-bv-low-k-runtime
```

Fresh artifacts at
`.fullmag/reports/frequency-domain-eigen-dispersion-de-bv-low-k-runtime/artifacts`
publish `requested_execution.include_demag=true`,
`validation.dispersion_frequency_source="analytic_reference_model"`,
`validation.dispersion_reference_model="kalinikos_slab_n0"`, and
`validation.dynamic_demag_operator_source="analytic_thin_film_de_bv_reference_not_fem_demag_k"`.
The direct validator also passes:

```bash
python3 scripts/verify_fem_frequency_domain_eigen_artifacts.py --require-low-k-de-bv-analytic-dispersion .fullmag/reports/frequency-domain-eigen-dispersion-de-bv-low-k-runtime/artifacts
```

### What we can calculate now

We can calculate a narrow DE/BV dispersion artifact and regenerate
`examples/dyspersje.png` through the CPU/reference analytic Kalinikos `n=0`
adapter. This is useful as:

- a publication-shaped DE/BV acceptance fixture,
- an end-to-end artifact/plot/Control Room contract check,
- a guard that the runtime preserves the requested DE/BV geometry, `k` range,
  `0..5 GHz` window, analytic model, and error columns.

This is not yet production numerical FEM dispersion with dynamic demag-k.

### What still must be done

1. Implement M10 dynamic Floquet/PBC demag for eigen: phase-aware
   `delta_m/delta_phi` coupling, boundary semantics, operator payload, and
   provenance. This is the real blocker for production DE/BV physical
   dispersion.
2. Replace the analytic-reference DE/BV frequency source with numerical modal
   eigenfrequencies for the production CPU lane, while keeping the analytic
   curve as comparison. At that point the manifest must switch to
   `dispersion_frequency_source="numeric_modal_solver_with_analytic_comparison"`
   and must not set `dispersion_reference_model` unless the solver is actually
   an analytic adapter.
3. Add convergence/mesh tests for the DE/BV fixture: thickness refinement,
   in-plane cell refinement, airbox/z-padding where demag is enabled, and
   sensitivity of the relative-error tolerance.
4. Add CPU production sparse/matrix-free selected-spectrum coverage for the
   DE/BV window, including residuals, normalization, damping/linewidth, branch
   tracking, and window-completeness policy.
5. Keep GPU modal dispersion unavailable until a native GPU modal eigensolver
   and matching Floquet/dynamic-demag operator exist. Forced GPU modal
   dispersion must continue to fail explicitly.
6. Extend Control Room only after the backend artifact contract is stable:
   show frequency source, analytic reference model, demag-operator source,
   DE/BV scenario mapping, and relative-error summaries without implying
   production demag-k when the source is analytic.

### Resume plan

When work resumes, continue in this order:

1. Add the native CPU modal contract test that rejects `include_demag=true`
   plus nonzero-k Floquet unless the request carries a real dynamic demag-k
   operator payload. This prevents the existing
   `payload_kind="bloch_floquet_tangent_operator"` from being over-read as
   full demag support.
2. Add the native unavailable diagnostic for that case with a stable reason
   such as `production_cpu_modal_dynamic_demag_k_operator_missing`, required
   contract `bloch_floquet_tangent_operator_with_dynamic_demag_k`, and required
   demag payload kind `dynamic_demag_k_operator`.
3. Wire the runner/artifact provenance so analytic DE/BV, no-demag production
   Floquet, and future numeric dynamic-demag-k production runs cannot share the
   same manifest source labels.
4. Only after the missing-contract gate is green, implement the CPU coupled
   modal demag-k operator against the physics contract in
   `docs/physics/0828-fem-frequency-domain-floquet-demag.md`.
5. Promote the new CPU lane only with managed/container proof through the
   matching `just` recipe and a validator gate that requires numerical modal
   frequencies plus analytic DE/BV comparison.

### Do not do next

- Do not promote the current DE/BV bundle as production dynamic-demag
  dispersion.
- Do not replace the DE/BV acceptance fixture with all-direction k maps.
- Do not count driven-response Floquet GPU smoke as modal GPU dispersion.
- Do not bypass the container-backed `just` recipe for native FEM runtime
  proof.

Regression-checked focused M2 missing-artifact cases (`6 passed`) and the full runtime artifact validator test file (`161 passed`), plus `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -m py_compile scripts/verify_fem_frequency_domain_runtime_artifacts.py scripts/test_verify_fem_frequency_domain_runtime_artifacts.py`. |
